# Outcome of every audited gap

One row per MISSING/PARTIAL gap of the audit (see `gaps.md` for the original detail), with its outcome after the fix campaign, the commits that name it, and the re-audit's evidence or what remains.

| c20-107 | medium | src/renderer/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx:27 | A render error in the app, a workspace pane or one tab is caught, and only that subtree shows a fallback | Deferred (GPUI limit) | a0005aef | A per-subtree boundary needs GPUI to recover from a panic in render or a listener. The unwind leaves the view's entity lease un-ended, so the entity is lost, and leaves the element and dispatch stacks unbalanced. On macOS it also crosses an extern "C" AppKit callback and aborts. That needs fallible render support in GPUI (~upstream change). Partial fix: p14-g's app-level crash screen with Reload Application. |

The app-level Reload Application (p14-g) is the partial. |

| c24-113 | low | src/renderer/components/FileViewer.tsx:612 | Each comment is shown with hideLineRef and delete | Deferred (GPUI limit) | a0005aef | This follows from the panel (c24-67). Electron hides the line ref because the comment sits right under its line. In Rust's panel, the 'Line N (side)' link (comment_ui.rs:67, Placement::Viewer) is the only way to tell which line a comment is on. Hiding it only makes sense once comments can sit inline, which needs Kit view zones. |
| c24-67 | low | src/renderer/components/FileViewer.tsx:396 | A line's comments open in a view zone after that line | Deferred (GPUI limit) | a0005aef | Still a panel at HEAD (file_browser_view.rs:1353 comments_panel). GPUI Kit's code editor (InputState) cannot reserve vertical space between lines: it has no Monaco-style view zones or block decorations, and the README Appearance section already names this limit. A full fix needs a vendor patch to the Kit editor's line layout, hit-testing and scrolling for block decorations. The partial fix, a 'REVIEW COMMENTS (n)' panel under the editor, already ships. |

| c25-50 | low | src/renderer/components/FilesystemBrowser.tsx:217 | A file's review comments are shown inline under their lines (Monaco view zones) | Deferred (GPUI limit) | a0005aef | Confirmed: comments are listed in a panel under the editor (file_browser_view.rs comments_panel). | c45-73 | medium | src/renderer/components/TabContentPortals.tsx:82 | Per-tab ErrorBoundary: a crash in one tab's view shows a fallback in that tab, logs the error, and the rest of the app keeps running | Deferred (GPUI limit) | a0005aef | Needs a capability GPUI lacks. Views render inside entity leases and the frame is not unwind-safe: catch_unwind around a tab's element would leave the leased view entity dropped from the entity map and the window's element/dispatch stacks half-built, and a panic inside a macOS callback aborts. A real per-tab boundary needs GPUI support for unwind-safe drawing with per-view poisoning. No honest partial fix exists: a panic hook could only log before the process ends, which is not the Electron behaviour. |

Catching one inside layout/paint unwinds through GPUI's window stacks (element id, text style, content mask), and the entity being updated drops out of the entity map (its lease is lost while panicking), so resuming is unsound. | c00-105 | low | src/applications/comments/renderer.ts:16-18 | Null or non-object Comments state renders nothing | Different on purpose | a0005aef | Electron's render returns null for a Comments tab whose saved state is null or not an object (applications/comments/renderer.ts:16-18), which leaves a blank, useless tab. Rust builds CommentsModel without reading the state (workspace_store.rs:862-865) and lists the comments, which is strictly more useful. README wording: "A Comments tab whose saved state is null or not an object still lists the comments (Electron rendered an empty tab)." |
| c00-122 | low | src/applications/customRunner/renderer.ts:38-45 | On custom-runner PTY creation write ptyId/ptyHandle/connectionId (even after dispose) | Different on purpose | a0005aef | connectionId: same reason as c00-18. Writing nothing after dispose is the ghost-writer rule: a disposed store must not write. The tab's initial state already carries ptyHandle (apps.rs:132-161), and create_pty is keyed by that handle (workspace_store.rs:1004), so the next store finds the same PTY again. README wording: "A PTY that finishes starting after its workspace store was disposed is not written to the body; the next store re-attaches it by its persisted ptyHandle." |
| c00-160 | low | src/applications/ghosttyTerminal/renderer.ts:37 | 'Terminal (Ghostty)' offered in the new-tab menu | Different on purpose |  | Natively the alias is the same terminal, so offering "Terminal (Ghostty)" next to "Terminal" would add a duplicate entry that behaves identically. Hidden on purpose (apps.rs:428 comment, test :745). README wording: "'Terminal (Ghostty)' is not offered in the new-tab menu or the default-application lists: natively it is the same terminal as 'Terminal'. Ghostty tabs made in Electron open as native terminals with their ghost icon." |
| c00-178 | low | src/applications/review/renderer.ts:19-21 | Non-object state or non-string parentWorkspaceId renders nothing | Different on purpose | a0005aef | Electron's isReviewState guard (renderer.ts:19-21) renders an empty tab when the state is malformed; Rust shows the review. README wording: "A Review tab whose saved state is malformed (not an object, or a non-string parentWorkspaceId) still shows the review; Electron rendered an empty tab." |
| c00-18 | low | src/applications/aiHarness/renderer.ts:60-65 | On PTY creation write ptyId, ptyHandle and connectionId into tab state | Different on purpose | a0005aef | Electron's ws.connectionId is a per-process random UUID (src/main/connectionManager.ts:509). If Rust wrote its own id, Electron's close() would use it (`current?.connectionId ?? ws.connectionId`, aiHarness/renderer.ts:82) and fail to kill the PTY on a connection it does not know. Leaving it out makes Electron fall back to its own connection. README wording: "Terminal, AI-harness and custom-runner tabs do not record `connectionId`: it is a per-process connection id that another app cannot use, and without it Electron kills the PTY through its own connection." |
| c00-180 | low | src/applications/review/renderer.ts:26 | Review's compare/merge parent comes from the tab state's parentWorkspaceId | Different on purpose | a0005aef | Rust takes the base and merge target from the workspace's current parent (workspace_store.rs:770-774, session_store.rs:946-959). Electron uses the parent stored in the tab when it was opened, which goes stale after a reparent, so Rust's choice is safer for merges. README wording: "Review compares against and merges into the workspace's current parent, also after the workspace is moved under another one; Electron used the parent recorded in the tab when it was opened, and treated a tab without one as top-level." |

README wording: "An AI-harness tab whose saved state lacks its sandbox settings still shows its terminal (Electron left the tab blank while the PTY ran)." |

| c04-51 | low | src/main/grpcClient.ts:268 | Each response message's serialized protobuf size is added to the connection byte counter | Different on purpose |  | Rust counts every byte read from the daemon socket (traffic.rs CountingStream, transport.rs:50), i.e. the real download including HTTP/2 and gRPC framing; Electron summed only protobuf payload sizes (grpcByteCounter.ts) and so under-reported small, frequent PTY output.
